1. How to use this manual

1.1 What this manual is, and what it is not

This is the User Acceptance Test (UAT) manual for SERK Construction OS. It is the human acceptance layer on top of the automated tests: a person with a real job title performs real work and decides whether the system does what we promised.

It is written from the Software Requirements Specification (SRS v1.0), not from the current build. Every test case names the SRS requirement IDs it verifies, and the expected results state what the SRS says the system *shall* do. If today's software behaves differently, that is a finding, not a reason to change the test. The SRS "Baseline" column (Exists / Partial / New) is shown in Appendix C only to help planning; it never lowers what must pass.

Screen names in this manual are deliberately generic (for example "the requisition screen"). If your screen uses a different label, write the actual label in the Notes box. That is a documentation finding, not a failure. Behaviour is what is tested, not menu names.

1.2 Who does what

The manual is organised by business workflow (the order a contractor lives it: win, plan, buy, build, bill, pay, close) so that a tester learns the industry while testing. Each test case also lists the role that performs it. Chapter "Role execution sheets" regroups every case by role, so a storekeeper can run only the storekeeper's cases, and an auditor only the auditor's.

Release tagMeaning (SRS section 9.2)
R1Financial core and procure-to-pay (general availability): identity, ledger, AP/AR, treasury, P2P, inventory, contracts-to-cash, workflow, audit, reports, exports
R2Project controls and commercial: budget revisions, forecasting, WIP and IFRS 15, subcontracts, claims, bonds, payroll, fixed assets, consolidation, tax returns
R3Field, documents and QHSE: mobile app, timesheets, equipment, document control, RFIs, quality, HSE, P6 integration
R4Intelligence and scale: document AI, predictive risk, portals, BI connectors, report builder, load and soak at enterprise scale

Run only the cases tagged with the release being accepted. A case tagged R2 is not a defect if R1 is the release under test; it is simply out of scope for that sign-off.

1.3 Test case format

Every case is a box with an ID, role(s), SRS references, optional "before you start" data, numbered steps, the expected result and a result column. Tick exactly one of Pass, Fail or Blocked.

ResultMeaning
PassEvery bullet of the expected result was observed exactly.
FailAny expected result not observed, a wrong figure, a control that did not refuse, or an unexplained error. Raise a defect (Appendix A).
BlockedCould not run (missing data, environment, access, or the feature is not yet delivered in the release under test). Say why; re-run later.

The grey "Why it matters in practice" line under many cases explains the real-world reason for the control. Read it: it is how you learn the industry, and it helps you judge whether a surprising behaviour is a defect.

1.4 Defect severity

SeverityDefinitionExamples
CriticalMoney, stock, security or audit integrity wrong; data lost; a segregation-of-duties, isolation or budget control bypassedA user approves their own payment; a posted journal can be edited; tenant A sees tenant B
HighA Must requirement blocked or wrong with no workaroundCannot post a goods receipt; retention calculated wrongly
MediumWorks with a workaround, or a wrong label, format or unclear messageRefusal does not say what to do next; wrong column in an export
LowCosmeticAlignment, spelling

SRS section 9.1: no Critical or High defect may be open at release.

1.5 Ground rules for testers

  • Never test on a live company. Use the UAT sandbox tenant loaded with the fictional data in chapter 4.
  • Record starting figures (stock quantity, budget remaining, ledger balances) before any money or stock test and the ending figures after it. Most expected results are differences against the start.
  • Posted documents can never be edited or deleted, by design. A mistake is corrected by reversal, credit note or adjustment. Plan the order of your tests and keep a list of document numbers (section 4.4).
  • Do not skip negative tests (things that must be refused). They are the controls the owners are accepting. A refusal must be explained in plain language that names the rule and the action that would satisfy it (NFR-USB-06). Judge every refusal by that standard.
  • When a case says "(checker)" or "second user", use a *different* person's login. Testing maker/checker with one login proves nothing.
  • Fresh MFA is required for sensitive actions. Have the authenticator device of each test user at hand.
  • If a case needs a feature your release does not include, mark it Blocked with the reason "not in release"; do not Pass it by inference.

2. Understanding the construction business (industry primer)

This chapter is for testers who are not from construction or finance. Ten minutes here makes every later test case make sense.

2.1 What a contractor sells and why it is hard

A contractor (here: Abay Builders PLC) agrees to build something for a client (the employer or owner) for a price, to a quality, by a date. Unlike a shop, the product is made on the customer's site, takes months or years, and the price is agreed *before* the true cost is known. Profit is the difference between what the contractor is paid and what it actually spends, and that difference is easily lost through a few classic failures:

  • Spending more than the budget (materials wasted, prices rise, rework).
  • Building more than the contract pays for, or not being paid for changes the client ordered ("variations").
  • Missing a contractual deadline for a claim notice (a "time bar") and losing the right to be paid.
  • Paying a supplier or subcontractor twice, or paying for work not done.
  • Cash running out because the client pays 45-60 days after the work while wages and suppliers must be paid sooner.

SERK Construction OS exists to make those failures visible and, where possible, impossible. That is why so many tests are about *refusals*.

2.2 The life of a project (follow the money)

StageWhat happens in the real worldKey recordsSRS area
1. WinFind a tender, decide go/no-go, price it from quantities and rates, submit a bid, win the contractOpportunity, estimate, BOQ, tender packageEST
2. BaselineThe agreed BOQ becomes the project budget; the work is broken into a WBS and cost codes; a schedule is builtBudget, WBS, cost codes, schedule baselineEST, ORG, PCC, SCH
3. BuySite asks for materials; procurement gets quotes, awards, issues purchase orders; subcontractors are appointedRequisition, RFQ, quotes, PO, subcontractPRC, SUB
4. Receive and storeGoods arrive, are checked against the PO, stored, then issued to the workGoods receipt (GRN), stock, issueINV
5. BuildCrews, plant and subcontractors do the work; the site records it daily; quality and safety are controlledDiary, timesheet, equipment hours, inspection, permit, incidentSIT, HCM, EQP, QA, HSE
6. Measure and billEach month the quantity surveyor measures completed work; the consultant certifies; the contractor invoices the clientMeasurement, interim payment certificate (IPC), invoiceCON
7. Pay outSupplier invoices are matched to orders and receipts; subcontractors are certified; payroll is run; treasury paysAP invoice, subcontract certificate, payroll, paymentPRC, SUB, HCM, TRS
8. ControlEvery month: actual cost vs budget, forecast to finish, earned value, profit recognised, books closedCost report, forecast, WIP, journal, period closePCC, EVM, FIN
9. Hand overPractical completion, defects period, retention released, final accountCompletion certificate, retention release, final accountCON

2.3 Four numbers every project controller watches

NumberMeaningExample (cost code Reinforcement steel)
BudgetWhat we planned to spendETB 9,000,000
CommitmentWhat we have legally promised to spend (approved purchase orders and subcontracts), even before deliveryETB 8,200,000
Actual costWhat has really been incurred and posted to the ledgerETB 5,100,000
Forecast (EAC)What we now expect the code to cost at the endETB 9,400,000

Rule of thumb: budget is a ceiling for commitments. The system refuses a new order that would push commitments above the budget unless an authorised person overrides it with fresh MFA and a reason (SRS R8). Commitment is checked at *order* time, not at invoice time, because by invoice time the money is already spent.

2.4 Money terms that confuse newcomers

TermPlain meaningTypical value
BOQBill of quantities: the priced list of every item of work and its quantityThousands of lines
IPCInterim payment certificate: the monthly certified value of work done, from which the client paysMonthly
RetentionA percentage withheld from each payment as security that defects will be fixed; released later5-10%
Advance paymentMoney the client pays up front to mobilise; recovered gradually from later IPCs; usually backed by an advance-payment guarantee10-20%
VariationA client-ordered change to the work that changes price and/or timeAny
Claim / EOTA contractor's request for more money or more time, e.g. because the client delayed access; has strict notice deadlinesFIDIC: notice within 28 days
Back-chargeCost charged to a subcontractor for something the contractor had to do or fix on their behalfCase by case
Three-way matchPaying a supplier invoice only if it agrees with the purchase order *and* the goods receiptAlways
WAC / FIFOWeighted average cost / first in first out: two ways of valuing stock when prices changePolicy per tenant
WIP / over-under billingWork in progress schedule: compares revenue earned by progress with amount billedMonthly
EVMEarned value management: measures cost and schedule performance with PV, EV, AC, SPI, CPIMonthly
Liquidated damagesDaily penalty payable to the client for late completione.g. 0.1% of contract value per day, capped

2.5 Who does what on a real project

RoleA day in their lifeWhat the system must help them doWhat the system must stop them doing
EstimatorPrices tenders from drawings and cost databaseBuild and version BOQsEdit a BOQ after submission
Commercial manager / QSMeasures work, values subcontractors, prepares IPCs, chases variations and claimsTrace every quantity to a contract line; track notice deadlinesCertify work they prepared themselves
Project managerRuns the site, schedule, budget and forecastSee ready/blocked work and cost to completeApprove their own budget increase
Site engineer / foremanRecords the day, requests materials, receives goods, inspects, reports incidents, often with no signalCapture everything offline and sync safelyCreate authoritative money or stock facts from a phone
ProcurementGets quotes, awards, places ordersCompare quotes, enforce rules, track deliveryAward to one supplier without justification; split orders under the approval limit
StorekeeperReceives, issues, countsKnow what is in each store and what it costAdjust stock without independent approval
Equipment managerAllocates plant, tracks hours, fuel and servicingCharge plant to projects by usageCharge a project without recorded usage
HR / payrollPays salaried staff and daily labour correctlyApply statutory rules by effective dateRelease payroll without a second approver
Accountant / controllerJournals, reconciliations, month-end closePost balanced entries; reconcile subledgersPost into a closed period; approve own journal
TreasuryPrepares and releases payments; imports bank statementsDual approval, payment filesRelease payments they prepared; mark paid without bank evidence
QA/QC and HSEInspect, stop unsafe or defective work, investigate incidentsBlock progress at a hold point; track actions to closureLose or quietly alter a safety record
AuditorTests whether the books and controls can be trustedRead-only access to everything, including the audit logChange any data
AdministratorConfigures roles, workflows, devicesCompose roles; revoke devicesHave financial authority just by being an administrator
Client / consultant / supplier / subcontractorReview and respond in their own scopeSee only their own recordsSee anyone else's data

2.6 Why so many controls? Typical frauds and errors

Each control tested in this manual exists because of a real way that construction companies lose money. Keep this list in mind:

  • Ghost supplier / bank-account substitution: a supplier's bank details are quietly changed before a payment run. Control: bank accounts are dual-approved with MFA and shown masked (ORG-03, adversarial case A04).
  • Split purchasing: one 600,000 purchase becomes two 300,000 orders to dodge a 500,000 approval limit. Control: split detection (PRC-08).
  • Duplicate invoice: the same invoice is entered twice with a trivial change. Control: duplicate detection (PRC-08).
  • Self-approval: one person prepares and approves their own payment. Control: maker/checker in the application and in the database (IAM-08).
  • Phantom stock or workers: counts and timesheets that do not match reality. Control: independent count approval (INV-03), supervisor-approved timesheets (HCM-02).
  • Over-certification: paying a subcontractor for work not measured or not inspected. Control: QS certificate by someone other than the preparer (SUB-02), hold points (QA-02).
  • Missed claim deadline: the right to extra time or money lapses. Control: time-bar register with alerts (CON-04).
  • Altered history: records changed after the fact. Control: immutable posted records and a hash-chained audit log (AUD-01..03).

3. The rules the system must keep (SRS section 3, in plain language)

The SRS defines nine invariants. They are the non-negotiable behaviours. Any failure blocks production approval. Each one appears below with what a tester should *see*, and the chapters where it is tested.

IDThe ruleWhat you will observeTested in
R1Every posted journal balances, uses whole minor units (cents/santim), lies in an open period, is immutable, and reconciles to its sourcesUnbalanced or closed-period entries are refused; posted entries have no edit or delete, only reversalFinance, Inventory
R2No one approves, certifies, posts or releases their own work (maker/checker), in the app *and* the database"You cannot approve your own document" with an explanation; same result if the app is bypassedIdentity, Workflow, Treasury, every approval
R3Only authenticated humans decide. Automation, service and AI principals cannot approve, post, pay or releaseRules and the assistant can notify or draft; the decision button needs a personWorkflow, AI
R4Tenant, entity and project data are isolated; exports show no more than the screenAnother project's or company's records simply do not appear, and a direct link is refused and loggedIdentity, Reports, Security
R5Exactly one effect per command or event, whatever the retries, duplicates or reorderingPressing submit twice, or a replayed sync, never doubles a payment or stock movementSync, Integration, Finance
R6Offline devices only prepare commands; conflicts are shown, never merged silentlyA phone can record a goods receipt offline, but the server decides; a conflict appears in a list for the authorSite and mobile
R7The assistant sees only what the user may see, uses only computed numbers, and cannot actAnswers cite sources; a number that is not in the facts never appearsAI
R8No commitment beyond the approved budget without an attested, MFA-backed overrideAn over-budget requisition/PO/subcontract is refused; an override needs fresh MFA and a reasonProject controls, Procurement, Subcontracts
R9Tampering is detectable and a backup restores to a consistent, verifiable systemIntegrity scan reports clean; a restored copy reconciles to the originalAudit, Operations

3.1 The standard document lifecycle (SRS 3.2)

Almost every controlled document (requisition, PO, invoice, certificate, journal, payroll, payment) moves through the same states. Learn this once:

FromToWho and what guards it
DraftSubmittedThe maker submits; content is frozen and attested
SubmittedApproved or RejectedA checker other than the maker; scope, limits, MFA if required; mandatory workflow quorum if published
ApprovedPosted / Released / CertifiedA further distinct person where configured; period open; budget and policy re-checked
PostedReversedA reversal document by an authorised person. The original never changes
Any open stateCancelledAuthorised cancel with a reason; commitments are released
Offline draftRecorded, Conflict or RefusedThe server re-validates on sync; a conflict goes back to the author

3.2 How to test a refusal properly

Whenever a case says "the system refuses", check all five points. Any missing point is at least a Medium defect (NFR-USB-06, EI-UI-02):

  • The action did not happen (no document, journal, stock movement or payment was created or changed).
  • The message is in plain language and names the rule ("Purchase orders above the remaining budget need an override").
  • The message names what would satisfy it ("Ask a user with budget-override authority to approve with MFA").
  • The attempt is visible afterwards in the audit trail (user, time, what was refused).
  • The same refusal happens when the action is tried a second way (direct link, API call, a second browser tab).

5. Identity, tenancy and access control (SRS 6.1)

Industry context

A construction company is a web of people with very different authority: a foreman may request materials but must never approve a payment; a storekeeper may count stock but must never approve the adjustment; an auditor may see everything but change nothing. Most losses in contractors come from one person holding too many powers, so this chapter is the foundation for every later chapter. If these cases fail, no other approval result can be trusted.

Three ideas to keep in mind while testing:

  • Who you are (authentication, MFA), what you may do (roles), and where you may do it (project scope) are three separate checks. All three must pass for every command and every read.
  • Administrator is not a superpower. An administrator configures the system but holds no financial authority unless separately granted (SRS 2.3, R2).
  • Humans decide; machines prepare. A service account or the AI assistant can notify and draft but can never approve, post, pay or release (R3).
T-IAM-01A second company can never see this company's dataR1
Role(s): UAT lead / Release engineer, Process owner / Workflow admin / Security admin  |  SRS: IAM-01
Before you start: Two sandbox tenants exist: "Abay Builders PLC" and "Zambezi Constructors PLC". Each has a user with the Project manager role. Note one document number from Abay (for example a purchase order) and its direct link.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Sign in as the Zambezi project manager.
  2. Search the document number noted from Abay and open the saved direct link.
  3. Open the supplier list, project list and global search; look for any Abay supplier (Nile Cement Distributors PLC) or project (Lideta Residence).
  4. Sign in as the Abay project manager and check the same items are visible.
  • The Zambezi user finds no Abay record in any list, search result or report, and the direct link shows a refusal that does not reveal whether the record exists.
  • The refusal is recorded in the audit trail.
  • The Abay user sees their own records normally.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
Why it matters in practice: A construction group may host many contractors in one SaaS. A leak of one company's bids, rates or supplier prices to a competitor is commercially fatal.
Note: Inspection evidence (separate from this click test): the vendor shows the automated cross-tenant probe results run as the tenant database login and as the schema owner (NFR-SEC-06, test ISO-T01). Tenant must come from the database login, never from a value the client can set.
T-IAM-02Sign in with company SSO, or with local credentials plus MFAR1
Role(s): UAT lead / Release engineer, Process owner / Workflow admin / Security admin  |  SRS: IAM-02, IAM-04
Before you start: One tenant configured with an identity provider (OpenID Connect or SAML). A second tenant has no identity provider.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. In the SSO tenant, press Sign in and complete the identity-provider login.
  2. In the tenant without SSO, sign in with the local username and password.
  3. Sign in as a user holding any approval, posting, payment, security or admin permission.
  4. Sign in as a read-only user with no such permission.
  • SSO users arrive signed in with their company identity and the roles assigned in the system (not roles claimed by the provider).
  • The tenant without SSO accepts local credentials.
  • Every user in step 3 is forced through MFA (passkey or authenticator code) before reaching the application; SMS is offered only if the tenant has enabled it.
  • Whether the read-only user is asked for MFA follows the tenant policy and is stated on screen.

Note: Inspection evidence: local passwords are stored only as Argon2id hashes (vendor shows the credential store design and a sample hash prefix; the plain password is never retrievable).
T-IAM-03Sensitive actions demand a fresh MFA, bound to that actionR1
Role(s): Accountant / Controller / Finance manager, CFO, UAT lead / Release engineer  |  SRS: IAM-04
Before you start: User fin.b (approver) is signed in. The tenant step-up freshness window is the default 10 minutes. A journal prepared by fin.a is waiting for approval.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Approve the journal immediately after signing in with MFA.
  2. Wait 11 minutes (or the configured window plus one minute) without signing out.
  3. Open another journal waiting for approval and try to approve it.
  4. Complete the new MFA prompt and approve.
  • Step 1 succeeds (MFA is fresh).
  • Step 3 does not approve; it asks for a new MFA challenge that states which action it is for.
  • Step 4 succeeds, and the audit record shows the MFA assertion time next to the approval.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
Why it matters in practice: An unlocked laptop left in the finance office should not let a colleague approve payments for the rest of the day.

6. Organisation and master data (SRS 6.2)

Industry context

Master data is the list of "things" every transaction refers to: companies, projects, suppliers, customers, cost codes, materials. In a contractor the quality of master data decides the quality of every report: a project with the wrong client, a supplier with two records, or a cost code that is not in the approved structure produces numbers nobody can trust. Master data is also where fraud starts (a fake supplier, a changed bank account), so it is controlled at least as tightly as a payment.

T-ORG-01The group is modelled with entities, regions, business units and projectsR1
Role(s): Accountant / Controller / Finance manager, Process owner / Workflow admin / Security admin  |  SRS: ORG-01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Open the organisation structure. Confirm E1 Abay Builders PLC and E2 Abay Plant and Logistics PLC exist.
  2. Open each entity's settings. Compare functional currency, tax registration, chart-of-accounts mapping and fiscal calendar.
  3. Create a region "Addis Ababa" and a business unit "Residential"; assign project P-101 to both.
  4. Change E2's fiscal calendar start and confirm E1 is unaffected.
  • Each entity holds its own currency, tax registration, chart mapping and fiscal calendar, editable independently.
  • The project belongs to exactly one legal entity and may be tagged with region and business unit.
  • Changes to master data are audited with before and after values.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
Why it matters in practice: A group often has a plant-hire company, a property company and joint ventures with different tax numbers; mixing their books breaks statutory reporting.
T-ORG-02The project master carries everything a project needs, with stage-gate historyR1
Role(s): Project manager / Project controls / Planner, Commercial manager / Quantity surveyor  |  SRS: ORG-02
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Open project P-101. Check code, name, client, contract type, location with GPS coordinates, key dates, project manager, phase and status.
  2. Move the phase from "Tender" to "Mobilisation" through the stage gate. Enter a reason.
  3. Open the stage-gate history.
  • All listed fields exist and are validated (for example GPS coordinates must be valid latitude/longitude).
  • The phase change is recorded with user, date and reason; earlier phases are still visible and cannot be edited.

7. Business development, estimating and tendering (SRS 6.3)

Industry context

Contractors win work by tendering: the client issues drawings and a bill of quantities (BOQ); the contractor prices every line, adds overheads, risk and margin, and submits a sealed bid. Price too high and the bid is lost; price too low and the contractor wins a loss-making job. Good estimating therefore needs (1) a maintained cost database of material, labour and plant rates, (2) unit-rate build-ups that show how each rate was made, and (3) a way to reuse the winning estimate as the project's baseline budget so nobody has to type the numbers again.

Two controls matter here: a submitted BOQ is frozen (you cannot quietly change the numbers you bid), and an approved BOQ becomes the budget through a controlled transition, never by editing in place (EST-02, EST-07).

T-EST-01Opportunity and bid pipeline with go/no-go scoringR2
Role(s): Business development / Estimator, Executive / Owner  |  SRS: EST-01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As est.a create an opportunity "Hawassa Hotel": client, source, value 80,000,000, probability 35%, bid due date.
  2. Score the go/no-go criteria (fit, client, competition, capacity, margin).
  3. Open the pipeline view and the bid calendar.
  • Expected value is computed as 80,000,000 x 35% = 28,000,000 and shown beside the value.
  • The go/no-go total is stored with the individual scores and who scored them.
  • The bid calendar shows the due date; the pipeline totals match the sum of its rows.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:

StepsExpected resultResult

  2. Try to change a quantity in the submitted version.
  3. Create version 2 as a copy; change one rate; compare the two versions.
  4. As est.a try to approve version 2; then as qs.a approve it.
  • Step 2 is refused; the message explains that submitted versions are immutable and that a new version is required.
  • The version comparison lists exactly the changed items, with old and new amounts and the difference.
  • Self-approval is refused; qs.a succeeds. All versions remain readable.
 ☐ Pass

Why it matters in practice: If a bid can be changed after submission, either the company cannot prove what it offered, or someone can alter it unseen.
T-EST-03Resource-based estimate: a unit rate built from labour, plant, material, productivity, overhead, risk and marginR2
Role(s): Business development / Estimator  |  SRS: EST-03
Before you start: Cost database rates: cement ETB 900 per bag, sand ETB 1,800 per m3, gravel ETB 2,200 per m3, labour ETB 56.25 per hour (day rate 450 / 8 hours), mixer and vibrator ETB 800 per hour. StepsExpected resultResult

  3. Price a BOQ line of 1,000 m3 with this rate.
  • Materials 9,130.00; labour 337.50, with productivity 371.25; plant 400.00; direct cost 9,901.25.
  • Overhead 792.10; risk 198.03 (198.025 shown to the rounding rule); subtotal 10,891.38; margin applied; unit rate 12,525.08.
  • The 1,000 m3 line amount is 12,525,080.00 (rate rounded first, then multiplied) and the rounding rule is visible.
  • Change the cement price in the database: a newly priced estimate changes, the already-approved version does not.
 ☐ Pass

8. Project controls: budget, commitment, cost and forecast (SRS 6.4)

Industry context

This is the heart of a construction ERP. A project controller must know, at any moment and for every cost code, four numbers: what was budgeted, what is committed (ordered but not yet delivered or paid), what has been spent, and what it will finally cost. Finance systems alone show only spent money, which is too late; spreadsheets show commitments but drift from the ledger. SERK keeps all four in one system fed by the same documents.

The key control (invariant R8) is that commitment may not exceed the approved budget without an attested, MFA-backed override. Real projects overrun because purchase orders are issued "because we need it", and nobody sees the total until the invoices arrive. Throughout this chapter the P-101 cost code 03-200 Reinforcement steel is used: budget 9,000,000, committed 8,200,000, remaining 800,000.

T-PCC-01A budget revision and a contingency draw-down are approved by someone else; original and current budget stay visibleR2
Role(s): Project manager / Project controls / Planner, Accountant / Controller / Finance manager, CFO  |  SRS: PCC-01
Before you start: 03-200 original budget 9,000,000. Contingency 99-900 original 3,000,000.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As pm.a prepare a budget revision: transfer 500,000 from 99-900 contingency to 03-200 with a reason. Submit.
  2. As pm.a try to approve it; then as fin.a approve it.
  3. Open the cost report for 03-200 and 99-900.
  • Self-approval is refused (step 2); approval by a different person succeeds.
  • 03-200 shows original 9,000,000, current 9,500,000; 99-900 shows original 3,000,000, current 2,500,000. Totals of the project budget are unchanged at 100,000,000.
  • The transfer is traceable from both cost codes and appears in the audit trail.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked

Why it matters in practice: Project managers are always tempted to "fix" a budget to make a report green. Showing the original beside the current exposes it.

T-PCC-07Cost forecasting: ETC and EAC by method, versions and an approved monthly cycleR2
Role(s): Project manager / Project controls / Planner, Commercial manager / Quantity surveyor, Accountant / Controller / Finance manager  |  SRS: PCC-04

StepsExpected resultResult

  2. Create a second version using the remaining-budget method.
  3. Create a third version with a manual estimate to complete of 4,000,000.
  4. Submit the monthly forecast; try to approve as pm.a; then approve as fin.a.
  • CPI = 4,500,000 / 5,100,000 = 0.8824; EAC = 9,000,000 / CPI = 10,200,000; ETC = 10,200,000 - 5,100,000 = 5,100,000.
  • Remaining-budget method: EAC = AC + (BAC - EV) = 5,100,000 + 4,500,000 = 9,600,000; ETC 4,500,000.
  • Manual: ETC 4,000,000, EAC 9,100,000.
  • Each version is stored separately; the approved version is the official forecast and cannot be overwritten. Self-approval is refused.

9. Planning, scheduling, progress and earned value (SRS 6.5 and 6.6)

Industry context

A schedule is a network of activities linked by logic ("pour the slab *after* the formwork is inspected"). The critical path is the longest chain of linked activities; any delay on it delays the whole project. Activities off the critical path have float: they may slip a little without harm. Planners build the network once, get it approved as a baseline, and compare reality against it every week.

Earned value management (EVM) answers two questions with one set of numbers: "are we on time?" and "are we on budget?". It compares what we planned to have done (PV), what we actually did measured in budget terms (EV), and what it actually cost (AC). Without EVM a project that has spent 70% of its budget and finished 50% of the work can still look "under budget".

T-SCH-01Critical path calculation with all four relationship types, lag, calendar and floatR2
Role(s): Project manager / Project controls / Planner  |  SRS: SCH-01
Before you start: Use a 7-day continuous calendar. Build: A (4 days); B (6 days) finish-to-start after A; C (5 days) start-to-start +2 after A; D (3 days) finish-to-start after B and after C; E (4 days) finish-to-finish +3 after C. Project starts day 0.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As pm.a enter the activities and links, then run the schedule calculation.
  2. Read early/late start and finish, total float and free float of each activity.
  • A: 0-4; B: 4-10; C: 2-7; D: 10-13; E: 6-10. Project duration 13 days.
  • Critical path A, B, D (total float 0). C has total float 3 and free float 0 (E's finish-to-finish +3 holds it). E has total float 3.
  • Changing B to 8 days moves the project finish to 15 and the critical path remains A, B, D.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:

10. Procurement and supply chain (SRS 6.7)

Industry context

Materials are typically 40-60% of a building contractor's cost, so procure-to-pay (P2P) is where most money is lost or saved. The chain is: the site raises a requisition; procurement sends a request for quotation (RFQ) to several suppliers; quotes are compared on price and technical compliance; an award is made; a purchase order (PO) commits the money; goods arrive and are recorded on a goods receipt note (GRN); the supplier's invoice is matched to the PO and the GRN (three-way match); and only then is payment prepared. Each step is a controlled document with its own approver.

In Ethiopia, import is a major route (steel, MEP, finishes) and brings extra steps: pro-forma invoice, letter of credit, shipping, customs clearance, and duty, freight and insurance that must be spread over the items as landed cost. This chapter tests both local and import procurement.

T-PRC-01The full procure-to-pay chain, each step a controlled documentR1
Role(s): Site engineer / Foreman, Procurement officer / manager, Storekeeper / Inventory controller, AP / AR clerk and supervisor, Project manager / Project controls / Planner  |  SRS: PRC-01, PRC-03
Before you start: Cost code 03-100 Concrete has budget available. Use cement as the material (50 kg bags).
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As site.a create a requisition for 800 bags of cement on 03-100 and submit.
  2. As pm.a approve the requisition.
  3. As proc.a create an RFQ to S-01, S-03 and S-04, record three quotes, do the technical evaluation and award.
  4. As proc.a create the purchase order from the award; as proc.b approve it.
  5. As store.a record the goods receipt of 800 bags against the PO and confirm it.
  6. As ap.a enter the supplier invoice; match it; as ap.b approve it.
  • Each step is a separate document with its own number, state and approver, and references its predecessor (requisition, RFQ, quote, award, PO, GRN, invoice).
  • Every approval was made by someone other than the document's maker. Budget commitment appears at PO approval; stock appears at GRN confirmation; the payable appears at invoice approval.
  • From the invoice you can navigate back to the GRN, PO, award, RFQ, requisition and budget line (AUD-06).
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
T-PRC-02An RFQ needs a minimum number of suppliers; a non-lowest award needs a reasonR1
Role(s): Procurement officer / manager  |  SRS: PRC-02
Before you start: Policy minimum 3 suppliers per RFQ. Rebar quotes per tonne: S-02 ETB 85,000; S-04 ETB 88,000; S-03 ETB 91,000. S-02 is technically non-compliant (mill certificates missing); S-04 is compliant.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As proc.a try to issue an RFQ to only two suppliers.
  2. Record the single-source justification "Only licensed distributor for this brand" and issue.
  3. Issue a proper RFQ to three suppliers; record the quotes and the technical evaluation (S-02 non-compliant).
  4. Award to S-03 (91,000) and then try to award instead to S-04 (88,000).
  • Step 1 is refused: minimum suppliers not met, stating the policy; step 2 is accepted with the justification stored on the RFQ.
  • The system identifies S-04 as the lowest *compliant* quote.
  • Awarding to a higher price than the lowest compliant quote (S-03) is refused until a written reason is entered, and the reason appears on the award and the control review report.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked

11. Subcontract management (SRS 6.8)

Industry context

On many projects subcontractors do most of the work: plastering, MEP, aluminium, tiling, landscaping. A subcontract is a small contract of its own, with a schedule of values, retention, an advance payment and monthly payment applications. Each month the subcontractor says what they completed; the contractor's quantity surveyor measures and certifies it; deductions are made (retention, advance recovery, back-charges, withholding tax); and only the certified net amount becomes a payable. Uncontrolled certification is one of the biggest leaks in construction: paying for work that is not done, not inspected, or done twice.

T-SUB-01A subcontract is a commitment against the budgetR2
Role(s): Procurement officer / manager, Project manager / Project controls / Planner, Commercial manager / Quantity surveyor  |  SRS: SUB-01, PCC-02
Before you start: SC-01 Selam Finishing PLC; subcontract value 20,000,000; retention 10%; advance 15% (3,000,000); on cost code 09-100 (budget 21,000,000).
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As proc.a create the subcontract with a schedule of values (for example: internal plaster 8,000,000; external plaster 6,000,000; screed 4,000,000; paint 2,000,000), retention, advance and scope.
  2. Submit; approve with the next level (not the creator).
  3. Check commitments on 09-100.
  4. Create a second subcontract on 09-100 for 2,000,000 more than the remaining budget and submit.
  • SoV total 20,000,000 equals the subcontract value; the screen blocks submission if they differ.
  • After approval 09-100 shows commitment 20,000,000 (remaining 1,000,000).
  • Step 4 is refused for budget, with the override route stated (R8).
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
T-SUB-02Payment application, certificate and payable arithmeticR2
Role(s): Commercial manager / Quantity surveyor, AP / AR clerk and supervisor, Accountant / Controller / Finance manager  |  SRS: SUB-02, SUB-T01
Before you start: SC-01 as above. This month's measured work 4,000,000. Back-charge accepted: 120,000. Fixture rules: VAT 15% on the gross certified value; withholding tax 2% of gross certified value; advance recovered at 15% of each gross certificate.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As qs.a prepare Application 1 for 4,000,000 measured work: link each line to SoV items and the measurement sheet.
  2. Include back-charge BC-01 of 120,000.
  3. Try to certify it as qs.a. Then certify as qs.b (the certifier).
  4. Open the generated payable.
  • Retention 10% = 400,000; advance recovery 15% = 600,000; back-charge 120,000.
  • Net before tax = 4,000,000 - 400,000 - 600,000 - 120,000 = 2,880,000.
  • VAT 600,000; withholding tax 80,000. Payable = 2,880,000 + 600,000 - 80,000 = 3,400,000.
  • Self-certification is refused; the certificate by qs.b generates the payable (supplier invoice equivalent) ready for approval and payment.
  • Cumulative figures after Application 1: retention held 400,000; advance recovered 600,000; work certified 4,000,000.
 ☐ Pass

12. Inventory and materials (SRS 6.9)

Industry context

Building materials are bulky, valuable and easy to lose: cement gets wet, rebar is stolen, sand is over-issued. A contractor runs a central warehouse and a store on each site, and moves material between them. Every movement has a document: receipt (goods in, against a PO), issue (goods out, charged to a project cost code), transfer (between stores) and adjustment (after a count). Stock is kept in a base unit (for example kilograms) even though it is bought in bags and tonnes.

Stock must be valued. When the same item is bought at different prices, the system uses weighted average cost (WAC) or FIFO per tenant policy; the policy decides what a project is charged and the stock subledger must always equal the inventory account in the general ledger, with zero unexplained difference (R1).

T-INV-01Receipts, issues to cost codes, and approved transfers between storesR1
Role(s): Storekeeper / Inventory controller, Site engineer / Foreman, Project manager / Project controls / Planner  |  SRS: INV-01
Before you start: ST-101 holds 1,000 bags of cement at ETB 900 (fixture). W-01 central warehouse has 3,000 bags.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As store.a record a goods receipt of 400 bags at ETB 960 against a PO into ST-101.
  2. Issue 300 bags to project P-101, cost code 03-100.
  3. As store.b create a transfer of 500 bags from W-01 to ST-101; have it approved by a second person; dispatch it.
  4. As store.a receive the transfer.
  5. Try to change a posted receipt quantity.
  • Receipt increases ST-101 by 400 bags; issue reduces it by 300 and charges 03-100.
  • The transfer needs approval by someone other than the requester; during transport the stock shows "in transit" (not in either store); after receipt W-01 is 2,500 and ST-101 increases by 500.
  • The posted receipt is immutable (correction by adjustment/return only).
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
T-INV-02Stock is valued by the tenant policy and reconciles to the ledger with zero differenceR1
Role(s): Storekeeper / Inventory controller, Accountant / Controller / Finance manager  |  SRS: INV-02, FIN-05, NFR-DAT-03
Before you start: Policy WAC. Start: 1,000 bags at ETB 900. Record S0 (quantity) and V0 (value).
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Receive 400 bags at ETB 960.
  2. Issue 300 bags to 03-100.
  3. Open the stock valuation and the reconciliation of stock subledger to the inventory control account.
  4. Switch a second test tenant to FIFO, repeat steps 1-2.
  • After step 1: quantity 1,400 bags; value 1,284,000.00; average cost 917.14 (917.142857 held at full precision).
  • Issue of 300 bags is charged at 275,142.86 to 03-100; remaining value 1,008,857.14 for 1,100 bags.
  • The subledger equals the inventory control account; the unexplained difference is exactly 0.00.
  • FIFO tenant: the 300 bags issued cost 270,000.00 (oldest layer at 900); remaining value 1,014,000.00 (700 bags at 900 plus 400 bags at 960).
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
Why it matters in practice: A wrong stock value means a wrong cost on every job and a wrong balance sheet.

13. Equipment and fleet (SRS 6.10)

Industry context

Excavators, cranes, tippers and mixers are expensive and idle plant loses money every hour. A contractor treats plant as an internal business: the plant owner (here entity E2) charges each project an hourly or daily hire rate, and the project pays it as a cost. This shows the true cost of using plant, makes managers hand idle machines back, and covers depreciation, fuel, repair and operators. Plant also breaks down: preventive maintenance by hours or distance is far cheaper than a breakdown during a pour.

T-EQP-01Equipment register linked to fixed assetsR3
Role(s): Equipment manager, Accountant / Controller / Finance manager  |  SRS: EQP-01, FIN-06
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Create EX-01 Excavator 20 t (owned by E2), TR-02 Tipper (hired in) and a leased item.
  2. For EX-01 link the fixed asset record (cost 6,000,000, residual 600,000, 5 years).
  • Ownership type, operating cost, hire rate and the linked fixed asset are stored. The asset register and equipment register show the same item.
 ☐ Pass

☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

14. Workforce, time and payroll (SRS 6.11)

Industry context

Construction pays two kinds of people: salaried staff (engineers, managers) and daily labourers who may be hired for weeks and paid by the day, often in cash or mobile money. Both must be costed to the project and cost code where they worked, otherwise labour cost disappears into overhead. Payroll rules are strict and change: income tax bands, pension contributions and overtime rules are set by law, so the system holds them as effective-dated data and a qualified adviser signs the rules before go-live (SRS D-04). Payroll is high-risk (ghost employees, inflated hours), so it is approved by a second person and every run produces journals, a payment file and statutory returns.

T-HCM-01Employee master for salaried staff and daily labourersR2
Role(s): HR / Payroll officer  |  SRS: HCM-01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Create an engineer (salaried, grade, bank account) and a daily labourer (trade mason, day rate 450, mobile-money number).
  2. Open both as a payroll checker and as a general user.
  • Contracts, grade, trade, rate, certifications are stored. Bank and mobile-money details are masked; viewing the full value needs the reveal permission and is audited.

15. Client contracts, revenue and claims (SRS 6.12)

Industry context

This is the "contract to cash" side: how the contractor gets paid. Each month the quantity surveyor measures the work completed, the consultant checks and certifies it in an interim payment certificate (IPC), the contractor issues an invoice, and the client pays after deductions: retention is withheld as security, and the advance payment is recovered gradually. Construction contracts are full of dates that cost money if missed: a claim for extra time or money usually needs a notice within 28 days of the event (FIDIC-style), otherwise the right is lost. Contracts also carry guarantees (bonds) that expire, price-adjustment formulas for inflation, and liquidated damages for lateness.

The whole chain must trace: receipt ← invoice ← IPC ← measurement ← contract line (SRS 8.1, AUD-06). Throughout this chapter the P-101 contract is 120,000,000 (excl. VAT); retention 5%; advance 10% = 12,000,000 recovered at 10% of each gross IPC; VAT 15% on the gross certified value (test fixture).

T-CON-01A client contract with lines or rates, payment terms and a contract policy is approved by someone elseR1
Role(s): Commercial manager / Quantity surveyor, CFO  |  SRS: CON-01, IAM-08
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As qs.a create the P-101 contract: lines or unit rates from the approved BOQ, total 120,000,000, payment terms 45 days, policy (retention 5%, advance 10%, VAT base gross).
  2. Submit; try to approve as qs.a; approve as cfo.a.
  3. Try to change the policy on the approved contract.
  • Self-approval refused. The contract policy is stored with the contract and used by every IPC.
  • Step 3 requires a new policy version approved by maker/checker, with effective date; past IPCs keep the old policy.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

  • The computation is by the server: editing amounts in the browser console or API has no effect.
  • Trace: from the receipt you can reach the invoice, IPC, measurement lines and contract lines.

16. Finance and accounting (SRS 6.13)

Industry context

The general ledger is the company's single book of record. Every business event (stock issued to a job, supplier invoice approved, IPC certified, payroll approved) produces a journal: a set of debit and credit lines that must be equal. Once posted a journal can never be changed; mistakes are fixed by a reversing journal so an auditor can always see what originally happened. Books are kept in monthly accounting periods; once a period is closed, nothing can be posted into it unless a senior person formally reopens it, and that act is itself audited.

Contractors have special finance needs: retention and advances (long-lived balances), revenue recognised over time by progress (IFRS 15) rather than on invoicing, foreign currency for imports, withholding tax and VAT, fixed assets such as plant, and several legal entities whose mutual charges must disappear when the group is consolidated.

Amounts are exact decimals in whole minor units (cents/santim): never floating-point.

T-FIN-01Unbalanced, closed-period, fractional and self-approved journals are refused; corrections are by reversalR1
Role(s): Accountant / Controller / Finance manager  |  SRS: FIN-01, FIN-03, NFR-DAT-03, FIN-T01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As fin.a prepare a journal with debits 100,000 and credits 99,000 and submit.
  2. Prepare a balanced journal dated in closed period P0 and submit.
  3. Enter an amount with a third decimal (100.005) on a line.
  4. Prepare a valid journal of 250,000 (debit 5100 cost, credit 2100 payables), approve with fin.b, post with fin.c.
  5. Try to edit and delete the posted journal. Then reverse it.
  • Step 1 refused: debits and credits differ by 1,000.00; step 2 refused: period closed; step 3 refused or rounded exactly per the stated rounding rule, never stored with hidden fractions.
  • Step 4 posts; the preparer, approver and poster are three different people.
  • Step 5: edit and delete are impossible; the reversal creates a second journal linked to the original; both appear in the ledger and net to zero.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Role(s): Accountant / Controller / Finance manager, CFO, Auditor (internal / external)  |  SRS: FIN-03, AUD-05
StepsExpected resultResult

  3. As fin.c try to reopen P1; then as cfo.a reopen it with a reason and fresh MFA.
  4. Post the journal; close P1 again.
  • Close is refused while mandatory reconciliations are open; the message lists them.
  • Posting into a closed period is refused. Reopen by fin.c is refused; the CFO reopening is accepted and appears in the control review report (who, why, how long it was open, what was posted).

Defect no.:

17. Treasury and cash management (SRS 6.14)

Industry context

Treasury decides when and how money leaves the company. In SERK the system never moves money itself: it prepares a payment instruction, two or three different people approve and release it, the instruction goes to the bank (as a file or by the bank's own portal), and only when the bank statement shows the debit does the system mark the payment as settled. This matters because "released" in the ERP does not mean "paid": a bank may reject a payment, and a fraudster could otherwise mark an invoice as paid without any money moving.

Cash is the lifeblood of a contractor. The client pays 45-60 days after the work, but wages and suppliers must be paid sooner, so treasury maintains a 13-week cash forecast that separates confirmed flows (approved invoices, certified IPCs) from estimated ones (expected IPC receipts, payroll, tax).

T-TRS-01Payments are prepared, approved and released by three different people with fresh MFAR1
Role(s): Treasury officer / Bank statement officer, CFO, AP / AR clerk and supervisor  |  SRS: TRS-01, IAM-08, IAM-04
Before you start: Approved payable to SC-01 for 3,400,000 (T-SUB-02). Dual-approval threshold 1,000,000.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As trs.a prepare the payment run containing this payable.
  2. As trs.a try to approve it. As fin.b (finance approver) approve without fresh MFA. Then with fresh MFA.
  3. Because the amount exceeds the threshold, have a second approver (cfo.a) approve with fresh MFA.
  4. As trs.a (the preparer) try to release; then release as trs.b (treasury manager) with fresh MFA.
  5. Inspect what the system now says about the payment and the invoice.
  • Self-approval and self-release are refused. Approval without fresh MFA is refused. Above the threshold two distinct approvers are required, and neither is the preparer or the releaser.
  • After release the payment status is "Released / sent to bank", not "Paid"; the invoice remains open until settlement.
  • Preparer, approvers and releaser are recorded; each decision shows the attestation/MFA time.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
Why it matters in practice: In every large payment fraud, one person could move money alone.
T-TRS-02Settlement comes only from the imported bank statement, by an independent personR1
Role(s): Treasury officer / Bank statement officer, Accountant / Controller / Finance manager  |  SRS: TRS-01, FIN-05
Before you start: Statement file from the bank (CSV, or MT940/camt.053 if available) containing the debit of 3,400,000 and a bank fee of 500.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As trs.c (bank statement officer, who holds no payment permission) import the statement.
  2. Check how the payment of 3,400,000 is matched.
  3. Try to mark the payment "paid" manually.
  4. Reconcile the bank account.
  • The import is recorded with file name, hash, user and time. The statement line is matched to the payment, which becomes Settled only now, and the invoice leaves AP.
  • Step 3 is impossible: there is no manual "mark as paid".
  • The 500 bank fee appears as an unmatched line to be coded; the bank reconciliation shows zero unexplained difference after coding.
  • An unmatched or rejected payment remains open, visible and cannot be silently closed.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:

18. Site operations and the offline mobile application (SRS 6.15)

Industry context

Construction happens in places with weak or no mobile coverage: basements, remote roads, rural sites. A site tool that needs internet is useless exactly when it is needed. The mobile application therefore works offline-first: the foreman records the diary, timesheets, goods receipts, inspections, incidents and photos on the phone, and the phone synchronises when a signal appears.

The key safety idea (invariant R6): a phone only prepares requests; the server decides. An offline phone must never create money, stock or approvals on its own authority. When the server finds that the request no longer makes sense (someone else changed the same record, the device was revoked, the quantity is no longer available), it returns a conflict to the author instead of guessing or merging silently. All requests carry a unique ID so a retry never doubles anything (R5).

T-SIT-01Daily site diary is approved by someone other than the authorR1
Role(s): Site engineer / Foreman, Project manager / Project controls / Planner  |  SRS: SIT-01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As site.a record today's diary for P-101: weather, 10 labourers, EX-01 6 hours, work done, a client instruction received.
  2. Submit; try to approve as site.a; approve as pm.a.
  3. Try to edit the approved diary.
  • Self-approval refused. The approved diary is read-only; additions are made as dated amendments.
  • The diary is linked to timesheets and equipment hours of the same day.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
T-SIT-02Only registered devices can sync, and a device can be revokedR1
Role(s): Site engineer / Foreman, Process owner / Workflow admin / Security admin  |  SRS: SIT-02, IAM-11
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Register site.a's phone to site.a. Record a diary offline and sync successfully.
  2. As adm.a revoke the device.
  3. Record another entry offline on the revoked phone and try to sync.
  4. Try to sync from an unregistered phone with site.a's login.
  • Step 1 syncs. Step 3 and 4 are refused with an explanation (device revoked / not registered) and no data from the refused sync changes anything on the server.
  • Revocation and refusals appear in the audit trail.
 ☐ Pass

☐ Blocked

19. Document control and BIM (SRS 6.16)

Industry context

Construction runs on documents: drawings, specifications, method statements, correspondence. The danger is working from an out-of-date drawing: a wall built to revision B when revision C moved it costs real money. A common data environment (CDE) following ISO 19650 holds every document in a state (work in progress, shared, published, archived) with numbering rules and metadata. Questions to the designer go through an RFI (request for information) with a clear "ball in court" (who must answer next) and a due date; delays caused by late answers become claims.

T-DOC-01The common data environment: states, numbering rules and metadataR3
Role(s): Process owner / Workflow admin / Security admin, Project manager / Project controls / Planner  |  SRS: DOC-01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Define a numbering rule: PROJECT-DISCIPLINE-TYPE-NNNN (for example P101-ST-DR-0001).
  2. Upload a drawing: the number is generated; set metadata (discipline, level, revision).
  3. Move it from Work in progress to Shared to Published; try to skip a state.
  • Numbers follow the rule and cannot be duplicated. States change only in order, each change recording user, date and, for publication, the approver.
  • Search and filters by metadata work.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:

  • Work packages that referenced B are flagged for readiness re-check (spec scenario A11).

20. Quality management (SRS 6.17)

Industry context

Quality in construction is managed with an inspection and test plan (ITP) for each work type, which lists hold points (work must stop until the inspector releases it) and witness points (inspector may attend). A concrete pour does not start until the reinforcement inspection is signed off; a defect found is raised as a non-conformance report (NCR) with root cause, corrective and preventive action and verified closure. Quality records also protect the contractor in disputes.

T-QA-01Inspection and test plans with hold and witness pointsR3
Role(s): QA/QC officer  |  SRS: QA-01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Create an ITP for "Slab level 2 reinforced concrete": steel inspection (hold), formwork check (witness), cube test (record).
  • Each point has a type, responsible role and acceptance criteria; the ITP is attached to the work package and versioned.
 ☐ Pass

21. Health, safety and environment (SRS 6.18)

Industry context

Construction is one of the most dangerous industries. HSE records must be timely and impossible to quietly lose: an incident hidden today becomes a legal problem tomorrow. Work like hot work, confined space, work at height, excavation and lifting needs a permit to work with conditions and an expiry. Companies report standard indicators: LTIFR (lost-time injuries per million hours worked), TRIR (total recordable incident rate) and severity rate. The hours worked come from approved timesheets, so HSE statistics depend on correct time recording.

T-HSE-01Incident and near-miss reporting, offline, with investigation and actions to closureR3
Role(s): Site engineer / Foreman, HSE officer, Project manager / Project controls / Planner  |  SRS: HSE-01, UC-12
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. On the phone offline report a near miss (falling object) with photo, then sync.
  2. Report a lost-time injury (2 days lost) from the web.
  3. As hse.a investigate: root cause, corrective actions with owners and dates.
  4. Try to delete or alter the original report. Close the incident after actions are verified.
  • Reports sync and notify the HSE officer and project manager. The original report cannot be deleted or altered; additions are dated entries.
  • The incident closes only when all actions are complete and verified.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
T-HSE-02Permits to work with approvals, conditions and expiryR3
Role(s): HSE officer, Site engineer / Foreman  |  SRS: HSE-02

22. Workflow, approvals and automation (SRS 6.19)

Industry context

A workflow is the route a document takes to approval: who must look at it, in what order, how many must agree (quorum), what limits apply and how long they have. In a contractor the same document (for example a purchase order) is approved by different people depending on its amount, project and type. If the route can be quietly edited, people will remove the steps that slow them down, so SERK treats a workflow definition as a controlled artefact: it is versioned, validated (a definition that weakens a control is refused), and when published as mandatory, the database itself refuses to let the document advance unless the workflow recorded its full quorum. Every human decision carries a cryptographic attestation from an isolated signer, so a decision cannot be forged even by someone with database access.

T-WFL-01Workflow definitions are versioned and validated; a definition that weakens a control is refusedR1
Role(s): Process owner / Workflow admin / Security admin, CFO, UAT lead / Release engineer  |  SRS: WFL-01, IAM-05
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As adm.a draft a purchase-order workflow with quorum 1 on POs above 500,000 (the policy requires CFO approval).
  2. Try to publish a payment workflow that removes the MFA requirement or the SoD rule.
  3. Publish a valid definition with SLA 24 hours then escalation to the CFO, and an expiry of 7 days.
  4. Open version history.
  • Steps 1 and 2 are refused by the validator, naming the control that would be weakened.
  • Step 3 publishes as a new version; publication itself needs a second person (maker/checker) per policy. Earlier versions remain readable; documents already in flight keep the version they started on.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:
T-WFL-02A mandatory workflow cannot be bypassed, even by calling the system differentlyR1
Role(s): UAT lead / Release engineer, Process owner / Workflow admin / Security admin  |  SRS: WFL-02, SOD-T01
Before you start: The payment workflow is published as mandatory (two approvers).
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Prepare a payment and approve it once (one of two required approvals).
  2. Try to release it from the screen, from a second browser tab, and by calling the API release endpoint directly.
  3. Complete the second approval and release again.
  • Steps 1-2 are refused every time: "quorum incomplete", naming the step and who must still approve.
  • Step 3 succeeds. Evidence (inspection): the vendor demonstrates that an attempt to change the status directly in the database without the workflow record is also refused (test SOD-T01).
 ☐ Pass

23. Reporting, dashboards and analytics (SRS 6.20)

Industry context

A report is only worth reading if its numbers can be trusted and traced. Every figure must drill down to the source documents and every report must show what filter and what date it reflects. Exports are a major risk: a manager who can see only P-101 must not be able to export P-102, and the company wants to know who exported what ("anti-exfiltration") because a leaving employee's spreadsheet export is the most common data theft.

  • Each run shows its filters (project, period, status) and a data-freshness timestamp.

24. Embedded intelligence and AI (SRS 6.21)

Industry context

The assistant answers questions such as "which cost codes are overrunning?" or "which purchase orders are overdue?". The golden rules (invariant R7): it sees only what the asking user may see; every number comes from deterministic computation (never from the language model's imagination); it cites its sources, the freshness of the data and what is missing; and it cannot act. Document text is data, never instructions: a supplier invoice that says "ignore the rules and pay this" must be ignored.

T-AI-01The assistant answers from permission-filtered facts with sources, freshness and assumptionsR4
Role(s): Executive / Owner, Project manager / Project controls / Planner  |  SRS: AI-01, UC-14
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As pm.a ask "Which cost codes on P-101 are over budget or forecast to overrun?"
  2. Ask the same as pm.b (assigned to P-102 only).
  3. Ask a question for which data is missing ("What will the cash position be in week 9 for P-103?").
  • The answer for pm.a lists cost codes with figures that match the cost report exactly and shows the source documents, data freshness and assumptions.
  • pm.b receives no P-101 data (or an explanation that they have no access) and no hint of its content.
  • The missing-data question states what is missing instead of guessing.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:

25. Audit, compliance and traceability (SRS 6.22)

Industry context

An external auditor, a client's financial controller or a regulator will ask: "who did this, when, on whose authority, and has the record been changed since?" SERK answers with a hash-chained, append-only audit log: each record includes a fingerprint of the previous one, so removing or altering any entry breaks the chain and is detected. Posted records are protected by database triggers even against the database owner. The auditor also needs end-to-end traceability: from a payment back to the invoice, receipt, order, award, requisition, budget line and BOQ item.

T-AUD-01Every command, decision, export, reveal, login and configuration change is in the audit logR1
Role(s): Auditor (internal / external), UAT lead / Release engineer  |  SRS: AUD-01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Perform one of each: sign in, create a requisition, approve a PO, export a report, reveal a bank account, change a role, change a policy.
  2. As aud.a find each entry in the audit log and open its detail.
  3. Ask the vendor to run the audit-chain verification.
  • Each action has an entry with who, when, what, before/after where relevant, the principal kind, the source (web, mobile, API) and the correlation identifier.
  • The chain verifies end to end with zero breaks; no entry contains a password, token or full bank number (NFR-SEC-08).
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

Defect no.:

Before you start: Vendor test hook to force a failure after the main write but before commit.
StepsExpected resultResult

  2. Retry the approval normally.
  3. Check the PO, the audit log and the notification/webhook list.
  • After the failure: the PO is unchanged, no audit record and no notification exist for it.
  • After the retry: all three exist exactly once.

27. Integration and interoperability (SRS 6.24)

T-INT-01Versioned, documented, authenticated, rate-limited APIs with idempotency keysR1

  • Each delivery carries a timestamp and HMAC signature that the receiver can verify; events for the same PO arrive in order; failed deliveries are retried with back-off and appear in the dead-letter queue; replay delivers once more without duplicate business effect at the sender.

29. End-to-end scenarios (SRS section 5 use cases UC-01 to UC-14)

How to run the scenarios

The earlier chapters test one control at a time. A scenario is a story told across several roles, the way a real month on project P-101 goes. Run scenarios in order UC-01 to UC-14 on a fresh copy of the fixture data: later scenarios reuse earlier documents (the budget created in UC-01, the stock received in UC-03, the certificate in UC-05). Every row has a person, an action and a figure to check. Where a figure is given as a formula, compute it from your starting numbers in the test data sheet (section 4.4). Tick the OK box when the row's result is exactly right; a scenario passes only if every row passes.

Keep one running list of document numbers. In each scenario the "who" column tells you whose login to use. If a step needs a second person, the row says so.
UC-01Win and set up a projectR2
Role(s): Business development / Estimator, Commercial manager / Quantity surveyor, Project manager / Project controls / Planner, CFO  |  SRS: UC-01, EST-01, EST-02, EST-07, ORG-06, PCC-01, SCH-02
Before you start: Fresh sandbox. P-101 does not yet have a contract, budget or schedule.
#WhoActionExpected result / figure to checkOK
1est.aRecord opportunity "Lideta Residence": value 120,000,000, probability 35%; score go/no-go and decide Go.Expected value 42,000,000 shown; scores stored.☐
2est.aBuild BOQ version 1 with unit-rate build-ups (concrete C-25 unit rate 12,525.08); map every item to an approved cost code; submit.Submitted version is frozen; edit attempt refused.☐
3est.a then qs.aest.a tries to approve BOQ v1; qs.a approves.est.a refused; qs.a succeeds.☐
4est.aRecord the tender package with bid bond 800,000 and expiry date.Bond appears in guarantee register with expiry alert.☐
5qs.a then cfo.aCreate the contract (120,000,000; retention 5%; advance 10%; VAT 15% on gross); cfo.a approves.Contract policy stored; self-approval refused.☐
6pm.aCreate the baseline budget from the approved BOQ.Cost codes total 100,000,000; every budget line shows its BOQ source; original budget cannot be edited.☐
7pm.a then qs.aDraft WBS/cost-code version and approve it; try to use an unapproved code first.Unapproved code refused; approved version usable.☐
8pm.a then qs.aBuild the schedule and approve Baseline 1.Baseline approved by someone else; project duration and critical path shown.☐
Result recorded in the data sheet: budget total ______ ; baseline finish date ______ ; contract value ______ .
Scenario result: ☐ Pass ☐ Fail ☐ Blocked   Date: ____________ Tester: ____________ Defect no.: ________

30. Adversarial and attack scenarios (Master Specification v2.1 section 19, mapped to the SRS)

Why this chapter exists

The best test of a control is to try to defeat it. Each case below is an attack or a nasty accident that a clever insider or a bad day could produce: forged approvals, replayed phones, cross-tenant probes, power cuts mid-transaction. The cases are grouped under the Master Specification's twelve scenarios A01-A12 and each is tied to the SRS requirements that make it testable. Most need the vendor engineer alongside a tester because they use fault injection or database access in a test copy only. Never run these on a production database.

T-ADV-A01Two projects try to reserve the same crane at the same momentR3
Role(s): Project manager / Project controls / Planner, Equipment manager, UAT lead / Release engineer  |  SRS: EQP-06, SCH-04, EQP-02
Before you start: Master Specification v2.1 A01. The SRS asks for resource reservation through resource loading and equipment transfers; if shared-resource reservation is not delivered, mark Blocked.
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. As pm.a (P-101) and pm.b (P-102) both reserve crane TR-02 for the same Tuesday, pressing confirm at the same instant (vendor script).
  • One reservation is committed; the other is refused with an explanation naming the conflicting project and dates. No double booking exists in any report.
 ☐ Pass
☐ Fail
☐ Blocked
Tester / date:

T-ADV-A02A maker/checker violation with a replayed or stale approvalR1
Role(s): UAT lead / Release engineer, Accountant / Controller / Finance manager  |  SRS: IAM-08, WFL-03, SOD-T01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Capture an approval request made by fin.b for journal J1 (vendor tool). Replay it for journal J2. Replay it after J1 has been changed.
  2. Try the approval as the maker through a second device.
  • The replay for J2 and the stale replay are rejected: the attestation is bound to the exact document revision. The maker's attempt is refused. All attempts are audited.

31. Non-functional acceptance: security, performance, availability, usability, operations (SRS section 7)

How non-functional requirements are accepted

Business users cannot judge encryption or throughput by clicking, so the SRS assigns each non-functional requirement a verification method: Test (measure it), Inspection (look at configuration, documents or evidence), Demo or Analysis (reasoned evidence from specialists). The UAT lead collects the evidence, attaches it to the case, and the relevant owner (security lead, operations lead, QA lead) signs. A case passes only when the evidence is current, dated, and from the environment under acceptance (a laptop result is not a production-like result).

Evidence rule: every case below says *what to attach*. "Trust me" and screenshots without dates are not evidence. A case with no evidence is Blocked, not Passed.

31.1 Security (SRS 7.3)

T-NFR-01All traffic uses TLS 1.2+ with HSTSR1
Role(s): UAT lead / Release engineer  |  SRS: NFR-SEC-01, EI-CM-01
StepsExpected resultResult
  1. Run a TLS scan against the web, API, portal and sync endpoints.
  2. Try plain HTTP and TLS 1.0/1.1.
  • Only TLS 1.2 or 1.3 is accepted; HTTP redirects or is refused; public endpoints send HSTS. Attach the scan report with date.

  3. Enter a document with two lines of 0.10 and 0.20 and read its total.
  • The split of 100.00 is 33.34, 33.33, 33.33 (or the documented largest-remainder order) and sums exactly to 100.00. Allocations always sum to the original amount. Totals are exactly 0.30, never 0.30000000000000004.
